\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$\dots$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr}}\end{array}\right\}

The corresponding four simple modules for
\mathsf{QP}_{2}(n)
:

\mathsf{QP}_{2}^{\emptyset}=\mathbb{C}\text{-Span}\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\scriptsize\vbox{\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\halign{&\vsquare{#}\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$\dots$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$12$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr}}\end{array}-\frac{1}{n}\begin{array}[]{c}\scriptsize\vbox{\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\halign{&\vsquare{#}\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$\dots$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$1$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$2$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr}}\end{array}\right\}

\mathsf{QP}_{2}^{(1)}=\mathbb{C}\text{-Span}\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\scriptsize\vbox{\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\halign{&\vsquare{#}\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$12$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$\dots$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr}}\end{array}-\frac{1}{n}\begin{array}[]{c}\scriptsize\vbox{\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\halign{&\vsquare{#}\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$1$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$\dots$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$2$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr}}\end{array}-\frac{1}{n}\begin{array}[]{c}\scriptsize\vbox{\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\halign{&\vsquare{#}\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$2$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$\dots$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$1$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr}}\end{array}\right\}

\mathsf{QP}_{2}^{(2)}=\mathbb{C}\text{-Span}\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\scriptsize\vbox{\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\halign{&\vsquare{#}\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$1$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$2$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$\dots$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr}}\end{array}\right\}\qquad\mathsf{QP}_{2}^{(1,1)}=\mathbb{C}\text{-Span}\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\scriptsize\vbox{\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt\halign{&\vsquare{#}\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$2$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$1$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$\dots$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt&\vbox{\hbox{\vrule width=0.4pt\vbox to10.0pt{\hrule height=0.4pt\vss\hbox to10.0pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt}
\vrule width=0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt}\kern-0.4pt\cr}}\end{array}\right\}

It is possible similarly construct the simple modules of
\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(x)

following the details in Section
3.2
about the representations of the half-partition
algebra. However, we do not carry out these constructions here because as we will see in the next
section, just before Theorem
6.3
,
\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)
is
isomorphic to
\mathsf{P}_{k}(n-1)
. Hence the representation theory of these
algebras is isomorphic.

6.
Quasi-partition algebras as centralizers

Daugherty and the first author
[
DO
, Section 2.4]
started with the definition that

(6.1)

\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}\!\left(\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\right)

where
\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}
is an irreducible symmetric group module indexed by
the partition
(n-1,1)
. In contrast, in this paper we start
with the generic definition of the quasi-partition algebra as a subalgebra of the
partition algebra and deduce the relationship as centralizer algebras
at specializations of the parameter
x
. The objective of this section
is to describe the centralizer algebras that correspond to the diagram algebras

\mathsf{QP}_{k}(x)
,
\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(x)
and

\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k+1}(x)
for values of
x
equal to a sufficiently large integer.

We will use this relationship to give a combinatorial description of how the
representation theory of these algebras are related. This will be described
in a diagram similar to a Bratteli diagram. Counting paths in this diagram
will allow us to compute the dimensions of the simple modules of these algebras.

Let
V_{n}=\mathbb{C}{\text{-Span}}\{v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{n}\}
, then it is well known
that
\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\cong\mathbb{C}{\text{-Span}}\{v_{1}-v_{n},v_{2}-v_{n},\ldots,v_{n-1}-v_{n}\}

and
\mathbb{S}^{(n)}_{S_{n}}\cong\mathbb{C}{\text{-Span}}\{v_{1}+v_{2}+\cdots+v_{n}\}
and that

V_{n}\cong\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{(n)}_{S_{n}}
as an
S_{n}
-module.
The action of
\mathsf{P}_{k}(n)
on the
V_{n}^{\otimes k}
is defined in
[
HR
]
and we follow similar
notation to develop the action of the quasi-partition algebras here.

For a diagram
d
, and integers
i_{r},i_{\overline{r}}\in[n]
for
1\leq r\leq k
, define the coefficient

(6.2)

\delta(d)^{(i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k})}_{(i_{\overline{1}},i_{\overline{2}},\ldots,i_{\overline{k}})}=\begin{cases}1&\hbox{ if }i_{t}=i_{s}\hbox{ if }t\hbox{ and }s\hbox{ are connected in }d\\
0&\hbox{ otherwise }\end{cases}~.

Note that we say that two vertices
s,t\in[k]\cup[\overline{k}]

are connected if they are in the same connected component in
d
.

The action of a diagram
d\in\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{k}
on an element of
V_{n}^{\otimes k}
may then be stated as

(6.3)

d\cdot(v_{i_{1}}\otimes v_{i_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes v_{i_{k}})=\sum_{1\leq i_{\overline{1}},i_{\overline{2}},\ldots,i_{\overline{k}}\leq n}\delta(d)^{(i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k})}_{(i_{\overline{1}},i_{\overline{2}},\ldots,i_{\overline{k}})}v_{i_{\overline{1}}}\otimes v_{i_{\overline{2}}}\otimes\cdots\otimes v_{i_{\overline{k}}}~.

With this action, each element
d\in\mathsf{P}_{k}(n)

is an element of
{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}(V_{n}^{\otimes k})
.
We will use the result that
\mathsf{P}_{k}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}(V_{n}^{\otimes k})

if
n\geq 2k
and
\mathsf{P}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n-1}}({\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}V_{n}^{\otimes k})

if
n\geq 2k+1
. For a clear exposition of these results, we refer the reader to
[
BH2
,
BH3
,
CST
]
.

We develop in particular the action of the projection idempotents

\pi^{\otimes k}
and
\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}
that are used in Equation (
4.6
)
when these algebras act on
V_{n}^{\otimes k}
and
V_{n}^{\otimes k+1}
.
We will consider the actions of these elements for
x=n
.

Recall from the end of Section
2.1

that
\mathsf{p}\in\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{1}
is defined by
\mathsf{p}=\{\{1\},\{\overline{1}\}\}
.
The action of this element on
v_{i}\in V_{n}
, for
i\in[n]
, using Equation (
6.3
) is

\mathsf{p}\cdot(v_{i})=v_{1}+v_{2}+\cdots+v_{n}~.

Therefore we have that

\frac{1}{n}\mathsf{p}\cdot(v_{i}-v_{n})=0

and

\frac{1}{n}\mathsf{p}\cdot(v_{1}+v_{2}+\cdots+v_{n})=v_{1}+v_{2}+\cdots+v_{n}~.

Therefore,
\frac{1}{n}\mathsf{p}
is
a projection operator from
V_{n}
to the subspace

\mathbb{S}^{(n)}_{S_{n}}
and
\pi=(1-\frac{1}{n}\mathsf{p})
is a projection operator
from
V_{n}
to the subspace
\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}
.

Theorem 6.1
.

For
n,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
using the action of
d\in\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)
as a linear transformation
on
V_{n}^{\otimes k}
,

d\in{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}((\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k})

and if
n\geq 2k
, then

\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}\!\left(\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\right)~.

Proof.

We see that
\pi^{\otimes k}=\overline{\mathbf{1}^{\otimes k}}\in\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)
acts as the identity
on
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}
. For any other diagram

d\in\widehat{\mathsf{D}}_{k}
,
\overline{d}=\pi^{\otimes k}d\pi^{\otimes k}

acts first by the identity on
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}
,
next by Equation (
6.3
)
(which may be an element of
V_{n}^{\otimes k}
and not
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}
),
followed by a projection into the subspace
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}
.
Moreover,

\overline{d}\in{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}\!\left(\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\right)

since it is a composition of three operations which commute with the diagonal action
of
S_{n}
.

Now take an arbitrary element
f\in{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}((\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k})
.
Since
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\subseteq V_{n}^{\otimes k}
, when
n\geq 2k
there
is an element
d\in\mathsf{P}_{k}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}(V_{n}^{\otimes k})
such that

d\cdot w=f(w)
for all
w\in(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}
and

d\cdot w^{\prime}=0
for all
w^{\prime}\in
the orthogonal complement
of
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}
in
V_{n}^{\otimes k}
.
Then
f(w)=\pi^{\otimes k}d\pi^{\otimes k}\cdot w
for all
w\in(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}

and therefore
f\in\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)
and we conclude that

\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}((\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k})
.
∎

Let the set
\{w_{i}:i\in[n-1]\}
where
w_{i}:=v_{i}-v_{n}
be a
basis for
\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}
.
We note that
v_{n}\in V_{n}
and it is invariant
under the action of
S_{n-1}
embedded in
S_{n}
where the element
n
is fixed by all elements of
S_{n-1}
.
Consider the embedding

\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\hookrightarrow\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}

that maps for
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\in[n-1]

w_{i_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes w_{i_{k}}\mapsto w_{i_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes w_{i_{k}}\otimes v_{n}

We assume that elements of

\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)
acts on
the space

{\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathbb{C}\text{-Span}\{v_{n}\}
.

Theorem 6.2
.

For
n,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, using the
action of an element
d\in\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)
on
V_{n}^{\otimes k}
, then

d\in{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n-1}}({\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k})

and if
n\geq 2k+1
, then

\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n-1}}\!\left({\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}\!\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\right)~.

Proof.

We extend the action from Equation (
6.3
) to an element

\overline{d}=\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}d\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}
for

d\in{\mathcal{D}}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}
and to
\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)
by
linearity. The action of a diagram
d\in\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}

also follows Equation (
6.3
) and because
k+1
and
\overline{k+1}

are in a connected component of
d
, then any
d
in

\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}
maps an element of

(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathbb{C}\text{-Span}\{v_{n}\}

to an element of
V_{n}^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathbb{C}\text{-Span}\{v_{n}\}
.
Moreover, the action of each
d\in\mathsf{P}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)

is an element of
{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n-1}}({\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}V_{n}^{\otimes k})

The expression in Equation (
4.1
)
allows us to deduce that
\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}
acts as the identity
on
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathbb{C}\text{-Span}\{w\}
.
Moreover, for
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\in[n]
,

\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}\cdot(v_{i_{1}}\otimes v_{i_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes v_{i_{k}}\otimes w)=w_{i_{1}}^{\prime}\otimes w_{i_{2}}^{\prime}\otimes\cdots\otimes w_{i_{k}}^{\prime}\otimes v_{n}~.

where

(6.4)

w^{\prime}_{i}=\frac{1}{n}(-v_{1}-v_{2}-\cdots+(n-1)v_{i}-v_{i+1}-\cdots-v_{n})

and hence it is a projection from
V_{n}^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathbb{C}\text{-Span}\{v_{n}\}

onto
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathbb{C}\text{-Span}\{v_{n}\}
.
With the isomorphism
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\cong(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathbb{C}\text{-Span}\{v_{n}\}
,

\overline{d}\in{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n-1}}\!\left({\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}\!\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\right)

since it is a composition of three operations which commute with the diagonal action
of
S_{n-1}
.

Take an element
f\in{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n-1}}({\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k})
.
Define an element
d

such that for all
w\in(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}
,
we have
d\cdot(w)=f(w)
and for all
w^{\prime}
in the orthogonal complement of
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}

in
V_{n}^{\otimes k}
, then
d\cdot(w^{\prime})=0
. Since

{\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\subseteq{\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}V_{n}^{\otimes k}
is an
S_{n-1}
invariant subspace, if
n\geq 2k+1

we can be assured that
d\in\mathsf{P}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n-1}}({\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}V_{n}^{\otimes k})
.
It follows that

f(w)=\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}d\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}\cdot w
for all

w\in(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}
and hence

f\in\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)
and we conclude

\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n-1}}({\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k})
.
∎

We remark that this isomorphism then gives us a better picture of the structure
of the half quasi-partition algebra
since we have as a corollary that
\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)\cong\mathsf{P}_{k}(n-1)
.
This is because
{\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\cong\mathbb{S}^{(n-2,1)}_{S_{n-1}}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{(n-1)}_{S_{n-1}}\cong V_{n-1}
.

Theorem 6.3
.

For
n,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
using the linear action of
d\in\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k+1}(n)
,

d\in{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}((\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n})

and if
n\geq 2k+2
, then

\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k+1}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}\!\left(\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}\right)~.

Proof.

Finally, we note that
\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}
acts as the identity on
the subspace
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}
and it acts as

\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}\cdot(v_{i_{1}}\otimes v_{i_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes v_{i_{k}}\otimes v_{i_{k+1}})=w_{i_{1}}^{\prime}\otimes w_{i_{2}}^{\prime}\otimes\cdots\otimes w_{i_{k}}^{\prime}\otimes v_{i_{k+1}}~,

where
w^{\prime}_{i}
is defined in (
6.4
)
and hence it is also a projection from
V_{n}^{\otimes k+1}

onto
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}
.

Recall from Equation (
4.5
) that a basis of
\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k+1}(n)

is indexed by diagrams
d\in\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{k+1}
such that
d
has no singletons in the first
k
positions.
Then any
\tilde{d}=\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}d\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}

acts on
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}
by first the identity
followed by a linear transformation to
V_{n}^{\otimes k+1}
followed by a projection
to
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}
.
We conclude that

{\tilde{d}}\in{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}\!\left(\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}\right)

since it is a composition of three operators which commute with the diagonal action of
S_{n}
.

Now assume that
n\geq 2k+2
,
if we take any
f\in{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}((\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n})
,
then since
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}\subseteq V_{n}^{\otimes k+1}

we can choose
d\in\mathsf{P}_{k+1}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}(V_{n}^{\otimes k+1})

such that for each
w\in(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}
,

d\cdot w=f(w)
and for each
w^{\prime}
in the orthogonal complement
of
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}
,
we get
d\cdot w^{\prime}=0
. Therefore, for all
w\in(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}
,

\tilde{d}\cdot w=(\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}d\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1})\cdot w=(\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}d)\cdot w=\pi^{\otimes k}_{k+1}\cdot f(w)=f(w).

We can therefore conclude that
f\in\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k+1}(n)

and
\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k+1}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}((\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n})~.

∎

7.
Formulae for the dimensions of the simple modules

The double centralizer theorem
[
P
, p.158]
implies that since

\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)
is isomorphic to the centralizer algebra of
S_{n}

when it acts diagonally on
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}
,
then the irreducible representations of
\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)

are indexed by partitions
\lambda
of
n
.
Moreover, the dimension of the
irreducible indexed by the partition
\lambda
is equal to the multiplicity
of the irreducible
\mathbb{S}^{\lambda}_{S_{n}}
in
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}
.

There is an explicit formula for the dimension in terms of Stirling numbers of the
second kind and standard tableaux given in
[
DO
, Theorem 4.6]
. That reference also states
the dimension in terms of a combinatorial interpretation of Kronecker tableaux
[
CG
]
.
In this paper we will rely on an interpretation that has been developed
more recently
[
COSSZ
,
HJ
,
BH2
]
in terms of set valued tableaux
in place of Kronecker tableaux
[
CG
,
BDE
]
or vacillating tableaux
[
K
]
.
We will use the result that the irreducible
representations of
\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)
have non-zero dimension if
|\overline{\lambda}|\leq k

where
\overline{\lambda}=(\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3},\ldots,\lambda_{\ell(\lambda)})
.

We will use the structure of the chain of algebras from Equation (
4.7
) in order to
determine the dimensions of the irreducible representations
of
\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)
,
\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)

and
\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k}(n)
.
We refer the reader to the diagram in Figure
2

to view a graphic representation of the
results that we present below.

Assume that
\lambda
is a partition of an integer

n
, then we will use the notation
\mu\rightarrow\lambda

to indicate that
\mu
is a partition of
n-1
and

\mu
is contained in
\lambda
.
Alternatively, if
\mu
is a partition of
n-1
, then

\lambda\leftarrow\mu
will indicate that
\lambda

is a partition of
k
and
\mu
is contained in
\lambda
.
If
\lambda
is a partition of
n
and

\mu
is a partition of
n-1
, then

\mu\rightarrow\lambda
if and only if
\overline{\mu}=\overline{\lambda}
or
\overline{\mu}\rightarrow\overline{\lambda}
.

Each row of the diagram in Figure
2
displays partitions
\overline{\lambda}

where
\lambda
is in the index set of the irreducible
representations of the chain algebras from Equation
(
4.7
). The rows of the diagram in that figure are color coded as follows:

•

The irreducible representations of
\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)
are displayed in
red
.

•

The irreducible representations of
\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)
are displayed in
blue
.

•

The irreducible representations of
\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k+1}(n)
are displayed in
green
.

We will describe the relations of the rows of this diagram in the results below,
but first summarize the relationship here.
There is an inclusion of
\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)
into
\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)

and an inclusion of
\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)
into
\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k}(n)

followed by a projection of the algebra
\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k}(n)

into
\mathsf{QP}_{k+1}(n)
. The diagram is similar to a Bratteli diagram except
that, because of the projection operation, the dimension is no longer the
number of paths in the diagram and is instead something slightly more complex.

The relations between the irreducibles in the rows of the diagram are summarized as follows:

•

(Equation (
7.1
)) Between the
\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)
and
\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)
rows
there is an edge from
\overline{\lambda}
to
\overline{\mu}

if
\overline{\mu}

=

\overline{\lambda}

or
\overline{\mu}

\rightarrow

\overline{\lambda}

(alternatively, if
\mu

\rightarrow

\lambda
).

•

(Equation (
7.2
)) Between the
\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)
and
\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k+1}(n)
rows
there is an edge from
\overline{\mu}
to
\overline{\lambda}

if
\overline{\lambda}

=

\overline{\mu}

or
\overline{\lambda}

\leftarrow

\overline{\mu}

(alternatively, these two conditions may be stated as
‘if
\lambda

\leftarrow

\mu
’).

•

(Equation (
7.4
)) Between the
\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k+1}(n)
and
\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)
rows
there is an edge from
\overline{\lambda}
to
\overline{\lambda}

but the dimension of the irreducible
\overline{\lambda}

is equal to the dimension of the irreducible
\overline{\lambda}

minus the dimension of the irreducible
\overline{\lambda}
at
k-1
.

The dimensions of the irreducible representations can be calculated recursively using the
following three results.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
n\geq 2k+1
, then
for
\mu\vdash n-1
such that
|\overline{\mu}|<k
, then

(7.1)

\dim\Big(V^{\mu}_{\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)}\Big)=\sum_{\lambda\leftarrow\mu}\dim(V^{\lambda}_{\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)})~.

Proof.

Since
n\geq 2k+1
we have from Theorem
6.2

\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n-1}}(({\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}{\mathbb{S}}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k})

and then from Theorem
6.1

\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}\!\left(\left({\mathbb{S}}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\right)~.

There is an inclusion of
S_{n-1}
into
S_{n}
and an inclusion of

\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)
into
\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)
.
This establishes a see-saw pair
[
GW
, Theorem 9.2.2]
between

(S_{n-1},S_{n})
and
(\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n),\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n))
which implies that

{\mathrm{Hom}}\!\left({\mathbb{S}}^{\lambda}_{S_{n-1}},{\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}{\mathbb{S}}^{\mu}_{S_{n}}\right)={\mathrm{Hom}}\!\left(V^{\mu}_{\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)},{\mathrm{Res}}^{\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)}_{\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)}V^{\lambda}_{\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)}\right)~.

Now the dimension of the space on the left-hand side is
1
if
\lambda\rightarrow\mu

and
0
otherwise.
∎

Let
\phi
be the
S_{n-1}
character of some
S_{n-1}
module
V

and
\psi
be the
S_{n}
character of some
S_{n}
module
W
.
In the following proof we will use the notation
\phi\uparrow_{S_{n-1}}^{S_{n}}
to
represent the character of the character
\mathrm{Ind}_{S_{n-1}}^{S_{n}}V

and
\psi\downarrow^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}
to represent the character of the
restriction
\mathrm{Res}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}W
. We recall that Frobenius
reciprocity
[
S
, Theorem 1.12.6]
relates these characters through the identity

\langle\psi,\phi\uparrow_{S_{n-1}}^{S_{n}}\rangle=\langle\psi\downarrow^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}},\phi\rangle~.

Theorem 7.2
.

Let
n\geq 2k+1
, then
for if
{\lambda}\vdash n
such that
|\overline{{\lambda}}|<k
, then

(7.2)

\dim(V^{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k}(n)})=\sum_{\mu\rightarrow\lambda}\dim\Big(V^{\mu}_{\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)}\Big)~.

Proof.

First, we remark that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ind}}_{S_{n-1}}^{S_{n}}{\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}({\mathbb{S}}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}

\displaystyle\cong{\mathrm{Ind}}_{S_{n-1}}^{S_{n}}\!\left(\left({\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}({\mathbb{S}}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\right)\!\otimes{\mathbb{S}}^{(n-1)}_{S_{n-1}}\right)

(7.3)

\displaystyle\cong({\mathbb{S}}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes{\mathrm{Ind}}_{S_{n-1}}^{S_{n}}\!\left({\mathbb{S}}^{(n-1)}_{S_{n-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\cong(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}~.

The isomorphism on the line labelled by Equation (
7.3
) follows from the “tensor identity”

[
HR
, Equation (3.18)]
. That is, for an
S_{n}
module
M
and
S_{n-1}
module
N
, the map

{\mathrm{Ind}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}\!\left({\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}(M)\otimes N\right)

\longrightarrow

M\otimes{\mathrm{Ind}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}N

g\otimes_{S_{n}}(m\otimes n)

\longmapsto

gm\otimes(g\otimes_{S_{n}}n)

is an isomorphism of
S_{n}
modules.

By the double centralizer theorem
[
P
, p.158]
,
the dimension of an irreducible
{\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}}_{k+1}(n)
module
indexed by a partition
\lambda\vdash n
is equal to the multiplicity
of
{\mathbb{S}}_{S_{n}}^{\lambda}
in
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}

which we have now established to be isomorphic to

{\mathrm{Ind}}_{S_{n-1}}^{S_{n}}{\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}\!\left({\mathbb{S}}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}~.

Let
\phi_{k}
represent the
S_{n-1}
character of

{\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}({\mathbb{S}}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\cong({\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}{\mathbb{S}}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}
, then
by Frobenius reciprocity

{\mathrm{dim}}(V^{\lambda}_{{\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}}_{k}(n)})=\left<\chi^{\lambda}_{S_{n}},\phi_{k}\uparrow_{S_{n-1}}^{S_{n}}\right>=\left<\chi^{\lambda}_{S_{n}}\downarrow^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}},\phi_{k}\right>=\sum_{\mu\rightarrow\lambda}\left<\chi^{\mu}_{S_{n-1}},\phi_{k}\right>~.

We have previously established by Theorem
6.2
that

\left<\chi^{\mu}_{S_{n-1}},\phi_{k}\right>={\mathrm{dim}}\Big(V^{\mu}_{\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)}\Big)

since (again, by the double centralizer theorem
[
P
]
)
the dimension of an irreducible
\mathsf{QP}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)
module
indexed by a partition
\mu\vdash n-1
in a centralizer is equal to the multiplicity
of
{\mathbb{S}}_{S_{n-1}}^{\mu}
in
{\mathrm{Res}}^{S_{n}}_{S_{n-1}}({\mathbb{S}}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}
.
∎

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
n\geq 2k+2
, then
for
\lambda\vdash n
such that
|\overline{{\lambda}}|<k
,

(7.4)

\dim(V^{\lambda}_{\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)})=\dim(V^{\lambda}_{\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}_{k}(n)})-\dim(V^{\lambda}_{\mathsf{QP}_{k-1}(n)})~.

Proof.

Since
n\geq 2k+2
, by Theorem
6.3
,

{\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}}_{k}(n)\cong{\mathrm{End}}_{S_{n}}((\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n})
,
then by the double centralizer theorem
[
P
]
, for a partition
\lambda\vdash n
,

{\mathrm{dim}}(V^{\lambda}_{{\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}}_{k}(n)})

is equal to the multiplicity of

{\mathbb{S}}_{S_{n}}^{\lambda}

in
(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}
.
Since as
S_{n}
modules,
V_{n}\cong\mathbb{S}^{(n)}_{S_{n}}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}
,
then

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\otimes V_{n}

\displaystyle\cong\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\otimes\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n)}_{S_{n}}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\cong\left(\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathbb{S}^{(n)}_{S_{n}}\right)\oplus\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k+1}

\displaystyle\cong\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k}\oplus\left(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}}\right)^{\otimes k+1}~.

Since by Theorem
6.1
and the
double centralizer theorem
[
P
]
,
{\mathrm{dim}}(V^{\lambda}_{{\mathsf{QP}}_{k}(n)})

is equal to the multiplicity of
{\mathbb{S}}_{S_{n}}^{\lambda}
in

(\mathbb{S}^{(n-1,1)}_{S_{n}})^{\otimes k}
, it follows that

(7.5)

{\mathrm{dim}}(V^{\lambda}_{{\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}}_{k}(n)})={\mathrm{dim}}(V^{\lambda}_{{\mathsf{QP}}_{k}(n)})+{\mathrm{dim}}(V^{\lambda}_{{\mathsf{QP}}_{k+1}(n)})~.

∎

Figure 2.
A Bratteli diagram showing the relations of the
tower of algebras in Equation (
4.7
).

7.1.
A combinatorial interpretation for the dimensions of the irreducibles

As a corollary we can give a combinatorial interpretation for the
dimension of an irreducible representation of each of the algebras.
The dimensions are expressed in terms of set valued tableaux
from Definition
3.6
.

Corollary 7.4
.

Combinatorial interpretation for dimensions of irreducible representations.

(1)

Let
k>0
and
n\geq 2k+1
and
\lambda\vdash n
,
then
{\mathrm{dim}}(V^{\lambda}_{{\mathsf{QP}}_{k}(n)})

is equal to the number of
[k]
-set valued tableaux of shape
\lambda

where the non-empty sets in the first row must be larger than
1
.

(2)

Let
k>0
and
n\geq 2k+1
and
\mu\vdash n-1
,
then
{\mathrm{dim}}(V^{\mu}_{{\mathsf{QP}}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)})

is equal to the number of pairs consisting of a cell containing
a subset
S\cup\{k+1\}
with
S\subseteq[k]

and a
([k]-S)
-set valued tableau of shape
\mu

where the non-empty sets in the first row must be larger than
1
.

(3)

Let
k>0
and
n\geq 2k+2
and
\lambda\vdash n
,

{\mathrm{dim}}(V^{\lambda}_{{\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}}_{k+1}(n)})

is equal to the number of
[k+1]
-set valued tableaux of shape
\lambda

such that if
|S_{(1,j)}|=1
, then
j=\lambda_{1}
and

S_{(1,\lambda_{1})}=\{k+1\}
.

We will not include a detailed proof of this corollary here since
it follows by induction and using Equations
(
7.1
), (
7.2
) and (
7.4
) and either adding a cell or
Schensted row insertion of a cell with a set consisting of the values
that are with the highest label.

Example 7.5
.

Below we have displayed three set tableaux of
[9]
and the shape of the tableaux
are
(m,2,1)
for an appropriate integer
m
. The first and third satisfy
Corollary
7.4
condition (1) for
k=9
.
The second and third satisfy Corollary
7.4
condition (2) for
k=8

if the cell containing
9
is considered separated from the rest of the tableau.
All three tableaux satisfy Corollary
7.4
condition (3) for
k=8
.

59

1

6

\cdots

37

28

34

2

578

\cdots

16

9

148

35

7

\cdots

269

Using standard counting techniques, we can enumerate the objects in Corollary
7.4
.
For example, the dimensions in part
(1)
of
Corollary
7.4
are equal to:

(7.6)

\displaystyle\text{dim}(V_{\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)}^{\lambda})

\displaystyle=f^{\bar{\lambda}}\sum_{t\geq 0}\binom{k}{t}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{k-t}{|\bar{\lambda}|}B_{2}(t)

(7.7)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\geq 0}(-1)^{s}\binom{k}{s}\text{dim}\Big(V_{\mathsf{QP}_{k-s+\frac{1}{2}}(n)}^{\mu}\Big)

where
B_{2}(t)
is the number of set partitions of
t
into blocks of size
\geq 2
.

Equation (
7.7
)
follows from Möbius inversion
because when we count
the dimensions in Corollary
7.4
part
(2)

we deduce

(7.8)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{dim}}\Big(V^{\mu}_{{\mathsf{QP}}_{k+\frac{1}{2}}(n)}\Big)

\displaystyle=f^{\bar{\mu}}\sum_{s\geq 0}\binom{k}{s}\sum_{t\geq 0}\binom{k-s}{t}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{k-s-t}{|\bar{\mu}|}B_{2}(t)

(7.9)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\geq 0}\binom{k}{s}\text{dim}(V_{\mathsf{QP}_{k-s}(n)}^{\mu})~.

We note that Equations (
7.6
) and
(
7.7
) are equal to
the dimensions stated in Corollary

3.5
which is of a different form.

We do not include a corresponding equation for the dimension of

{\mathrm{dim}}(V^{\lambda}_{{\widetilde{\mathsf{QP}}}_{k}(n)})

because counting the tableaux from Corollary
7.4
part
(3)

yields a formula that also follows from
Equation (
7.5
).

The formula for the dimension of the irreducible
\mathsf{QP}_{k}(n)
-modules
stated in Equation (
7.6
)
seems significantly simpler than the formula in
[
CG
, Proposition 2]
,

[
Sc
, Corollary 3.4]

and
[
DO
, Theorem 4.6]

which counts “Kronecker tableaux,” the name that they give to paths
in the Bratteli diagram of the tower of quasi-partition algebras.
